# Claim ledger: estimation levels 1 to 3, audit round 2 (`estimation-1-3-r2`)

Blind read at commit `0116a24e`. One row per definition, result, method step, convention, graded key, slip, wrong option, hint, feedback line and explanation. Stage 1: full ledger.

Rows: 3875. Verdicts: correct 3859, wrong 0, imprecise 13, misleading 3, unverified 0.

The basis is given per screen or item and repeated on each of its rows. "script check" names a line of `docs/reports/estimation-L1-3-concepts-r2.py`. "Pyodide" means the page's grader run in node through `app/tools/code-runner.mjs` with the local Pyodide 0.27.7. Sources: Casella and Berger, *Statistical Inference* (2nd ed.); Wasserman, *All of Statistics*; Efron and Tibshirani, *An Introduction to the Bootstrap*; Hull, *Options, Futures and Other Derivatives*.

Rows per file: l1-sample-population 216, l2-estimates-vary 211, l3-right-target 207, l4-bias-against-variance 205, review-1 109, homework-1 112, problems-1 214, l5-which-fits 199, l6-maximising 206, l7-taking-logs 222, l8-volatility-mle 204, review-2 112, homework-2 97, problems-2 198, l9-how-precise 227, l10-catching-the-truth 228, l11-bootstrap 228, l12-updating-a-belief 224, review-3 117, homework-3 119, problems-3 180, fluency-l9-how-precise 24, fluency-l10-catching-the-truth 16.

| l6-maximising | `screens[3].widget.lines[1].text` | $L'(p) = k p^{k - 1}(1 - p)^{n - k} - (n - k) p^k (1 - p)^{n - k - 1}$, by the product and chain rules | correct | Casella and Berger Example 7.2.7; sign of k - np |
| l6-maximising | `screens[3].widget.lines[2].text` | $L'(p) = p^{k - 1}(1 - p)^{n - k - 1}\big[k(1 - p) - (n - k)p\big]$, taking out the common factors | correct | Casella and Berger Example 7.2.7; sign of k - np |
| l6-maximising | `screens[3].widget.lines[3].text` | $L'(p) = p^{k - 1}(1 - p)^{n - k - 1}(k - np)$, since $k - kp - np + kp = k - np$ | correct | Casella and Berger Example 7.2.7; sign of k - np |
| l6-maximising | `screens[3].widget.lines[4].text` | The front factors are positive for $0 < p < 1$, so $L'(p) = 0$ only at $p = k/n$ | correct | Casella and Berger Example 7.2.7; sign of k - np |
| l6-maximising | `screens[3].widget.lines[5].text` | $L' > 0$ below $k/n$ and $L' < 0$ above it, so $\hat p = k/n$ is the maximum | correct | Casella and Berger Example 7.2.7; sign of k - np |

| l8-volatility-mle | `screens[2].widget.lines[0].text` | $\ell = -\tfrac{n}{2}\ln(2\pi) - \tfrac{n}{2}\ln v - \dfrac{1}{2v}\sum (r_i - \mu)^2$, the normal log-likelihood with $v = \sigma^2$ | correct | Casella and Berger Example 7.2.11 (MLEs of a normal mean and variance; the variance MLE divides by n) |
| l8-volatility-mle | `screens[2].widget.lines[1].text` | $\dfrac{\partial \ell}{\partial \mu} = \dfrac{1}{v}\sum (r_i - \mu)$, since only the last term involves $\mu$ | correct | Casella and Berger Example 7.2.11 (MLEs of a normal mean and variance; the variance MLE divides by n) |
| l8-volatility-mle | `screens[2].widget.lines[2].text` | Setting it to zero gives $\sum r_i = n\mu$, so $\hat\mu = \bar r$ whatever $v$ is | correct | Casella and Berger Example 7.2.11 (MLEs of a normal mean and variance; the variance MLE divides by n) |
| l8-volatility-mle | `screens[2].widget.lines[3].text` | $\dfrac{\partial \ell}{\partial v} = -\dfrac{n}{2v} + \dfrac{1}{2v^2}\sum (r_i - \mu)^2$, differentiating $\ln v$ and $\frac{1}{v}$ | correct | Casella and Berger Example 7.2.11 (MLEs of a normal mean and variance; the variance MLE divides by n) |
| l8-volatility-mle | `screens[2].widget.lines[4].text` | Setting it to zero at $\mu = \bar r$ gives $\hat\sigma^2 = \dfrac{1}{n}\sum (r_i - \bar r)^2$, multiplying by $2v^2$ | correct | Casella and Berger Example 7.2.11 (MLEs of a normal mean and variance; the variance MLE divides by n) |
| l8-volatility-mle | `screens[2].widget.lines[5].text` | $\dfrac{\partial \ell}{\partial v} = \dfrac{n}{2v^2}(\hat\sigma^2 - v)$: positive below $\hat\sigma^2$ and negative above, so it is a maximum | correct | Casella and Berger Example 7.2.11 (MLEs of a normal mean and variance; the variance MLE divides by n) |
| l8-volatility-mle | `screens[2].hints[0].text` | Start from $\ell$, then deal with $\mu$ first. | correct | Casella and Berger Example 7.2.11 (MLEs of a normal mean and variance; the variance MLE divides by n) |

| l8-volatility-mle | `screens[2].hints[2].text` | Order: $\ell$, $\partial/\partial\mu$, solve for $\hat\mu$, $\partial/\partial v$, solve for $\hat\sigma^2$, then the sign check. | correct | Casella and Berger Example 7.2.11 (MLEs of a normal mean and variance; the variance MLE divides by n) |
| l8-volatility-mle | `screens[2].feedback.correct` | $\hat\mu = \bar r$ and $\hat\sigma^2 = \frac{1}{n}\sum (r_i - \bar r)^2$. | correct | Casella and Berger Example 7.2.11 (MLEs of a normal mean and variance; the variance MLE divides by n) |
| l8-volatility-mle | `screens[2].feedback.generic` | Not quite right. Solve for $\hat\mu$ before using it in the $v$ equation. | correct | Casella and Berger Example 7.2.11 (MLEs of a normal mean and variance; the variance MLE divides by n) |

| homework-2 | `items[6].widget.options[0].correct` | keyed right: $\hat h = 1$, yet $h = 0.8$ still gives the record a chance of 0.17 | correct | h^8 increasing; 0.8^8 = 0.17 |
| homework-2 | `items[6].widget.options[1].text` | There is no MLE, because $L'(h) = 8h^7$ is never zero inside | correct | h^8 increasing; 0.8^8 = 0.17 |
| homework-2 | `items[6].widget.options[1].why` | You looked only for a flat tangent. $L(h) = h^8$ rises to the boundary, so $\hat h = 1$. | correct | h^8 increasing; 0.8^8 = 0.17 |
| homework-2 | `items[6].widget.options[2].text` | $\hat h = 8/9$, from the formula $k/(n + 1)$ | correct | h^8 increasing; 0.8^8 = 0.17 |
| homework-2 | `items[6].widget.options[2].why` | The MLE is $k/n = 8/8$. Formulas that pad the counts are other estimators, not the maximiser of $L$. | correct | h^8 increasing; 0.8^8 = 0.17 |

| homework-2 | `items[6].widget.options[3].why` | The MLE is 1, but $L(0.8) = 0.8^8 = 0.17$. Eight trades cannot rule out plenty of losing trades to come. | correct | h^8 increasing; 0.8^8 = 0.17 |
| homework-2 | `items[6].feedback.correct` | The MLE sits at the boundary and overstates certainty. | correct | h^8 increasing; 0.8^8 = 0.17 |
| homework-2 | `items[6].feedback.generic` | Not quite right. Write $L(h) = h^8$ and look at its whole graph. | correct | h^8 increasing; 0.8^8 = 0.17 |
